# Live World gait-retarget evidence

Retail `models.pck` read directly. Matrices are the ones the viewer's `Mat4::from_cols_array` sees (raw BON floats), so a `det` below zero means an improper (reflected) rest basis and `ortho` above zero means the columns are not orthonormal (shear or scale mixed into rest).

## Clip content probe (shipped gender donors, window-restricted)

What each locomotion slice actually contains INSIDE its authored clip window - not over the whole master track. The combined actions are windows into shared master recordings, so whole-track statistics describe jumps and falls, not the walk the game plays. `root`/`pelvis` are the authored position-key axis ranges within the window next to the bone's rest translation; the deltas are the authored parent-space swing angle in degrees inside the window. An authored root/pelvis drop or folded thigh reads as a crouch or a sit in game.

## Summary table

`player *` columns use the shipped core-coverage gate on retargeting (formula A rebased into each target's parent frame: Rm = M D M^-1 T with M = F_t^-1 F_s over rest parent-frame rotations) with the gender-correct player donor. `guard *` columns keep the legacy 70% gate (Guard rigs, the old behaviour) for contrast. `max chain gap` is the largest parent-frame divergence over mapped locomotion bones for the same-gender player donor; it is diagnostic only and never rejects a clip.

## Runtime selection mirror (per resident, gender donor)

What the game would actually play for each numbered resident rig, in the game's selection order (generic cycle names first, then file order), with the `visibly_moves` gate evaluated on the retargeted pose exactly where the game evaluates it. `vise` is a bone-level proxy strictly more sensitive than the game's 8-vertices-per-mesh vertex hash; `max bone move` is the largest mapped-bone translation between the clip start and the sampled offsets, in millimetres. When nothing is SELECTED the resident draws its bind pose while sliding along its route - the in-game 'stopped walking'.
